OpenHands resolver: the trusted pre-push gate's failing-first runs, planted defects, reader cross-check
and local rehearsal (decision record amendment of 2026-10-04, option 1 with trusted pre-push
enforcement; docs/decisions/2026-09-28-openhands-resolver-isolation.md).

Evidence classes (docs/acceptance-evidence-policy.md): local integration checks and synthetic
fixtures. None of this is upstream acceptance, a live resolver run, a container, a model call or a
GitHub write. The rehearsal in part 4 runs only the gate, on local clones.

Host tools: CPython 3.13.15 runs the tests; /usr/bin/python3 3.12.3 with PyYAML 6.0.1 runs only
the reader cross-check of part 3; git 2.43.0; zizmor 1.30.1, the version that
.github/requirements-ci.txt pins for CI. Every run used TMPDIR=/var/tmp/489-gate,
PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 and nice -n 19. Paths below are repository-relative or <scratch>.

`changes` step in adoption-bootstrap.yml compares `git diff --name-only` output with two bash

naming `tests/` also relabelled the discovery prefix `tests` as ci_named, because

9. Acceptance probe on 7c1d24cc5, 2026-10-04 (fix on top of 7c1d24cc5)

The acceptance of 7c1d24cc5 (10:43:06Z to 10:45:09Z) passed. A review of the new execution model
then raised a specific gap: GateReads.executed read the arguments of an executing call one node at
a time, so an argv list bound to a name (cmd = [sys.executable, script], then subprocess.run(cmd))
was never looked into. A probe compared the committed derivation on the 7c1d24cc5 tree with
widened ones, alone and combined:
- A: look into literal containers in an executing call's arguments;
- B: count a local built from a parameter as the parameter when finding wrappers;
- C: read a whitespace string in an executing call with names_in_text, the run-step rule;
- E: resolve each followed Python file's imports through the directories gate code puts on
  sys.path as well.
                       followed  protected  outside evidence/, tests/, .github/  unresolved
committed (7c1d24cc5)  42        7,617      632                                  none
A                      49        7,621      636                                  none
A and C                49        7,621      636                                  none
A and B                49        7,621      636                                  scripts/release_due.py:194
E                      49        7,621      636                                  none
A and E                51        7,621      636                                  none
A, C and E             51        7,621      636                                  none
The 4 files every variant protects again are those part 8 says the change unprotected:
blueprints/convergence-practice/service-reboot/plan.json,
blueprints/us-equities/research-evaluation/receipt.json, catalogs/foundation/automation.json and
catalogs/us-equities/runtime-target.json. Part 8's attribution was wrong. validate.yml runs
scripts/verdict_review_gate.py, which puts tools/sota-convergence on sys.path (lines 104-105) and
imports record_verdicts (line 121). record_verdicts imports export_isolation_check lazily (line
1222), which imports blind_checkout and lane_packets lazily (lines 432, 454, 486-487). Those modules
are gate code, and they read the 4 files (blind_checkout.py lines 194 and 207;
export_isolation_check.py lines 71 and 73). patch_policy.python_references turns a sys.path
directory into a directory rule: tools/sota-convergence was protected as code, but what its modules
read was not followed. Round 3's over-following had covered these files by accident, since
verdict_review_gate.py spells the scripts' paths in TRUST_PATHS. E is the actual route. A reaches
them only because changed_paths passes TRUST_PATHS to git as pathspecs, and git counts as an
executor; that is a false positive, but in the safe direction.

Adopted, on top of 7c1d24cc5: A, the container rule (GateReads._leaf_values), and E, imports
through sys.path directories (GateReads.imported_modules, push_gate CiProtected.syspath, a fixpoint
in _add_gate_reads over the directories found). B is not adopted, because it makes every push
refused. scripts/release_due.py's git(*args) counts as running code, and mentioned() passes a
derived path to git check-ignore, which only reads it. C changes nothing on this repository and is
not adopted. Both are residuals in the decision record. On the merged tree with the fix: 552
derived files, 18 prefixes and 10 globs, 7,621 of 10,591 files protected (72.0%), 636 of 3,447
outside evidence/, tests/ and .github/ (18.5%), 14 of 2,608 in blueprints/, nothing unresolved,
3.52 s to derive one tree.

7c1d24cc5.

13. Main-introduced literal-container blocker: final micro-fix exception (2026-10-04)

The coordinator authorized this exception because main 89cf253c0 introduced the pattern at tests/test_new_host_grand_list.py:115; it is not a new review finding. No further repair round follows. Any new finding remains a residual for a follow-up PR.

Offline search-first quick-mode findings and sources: the existing GateReads._elements, _mapping_elements and _at already model literal dict items/values/keys, tuple unpacking and literal list/tuple iteration. The missing link was from-package module aliases: `from scripts import new_host_grand_list as g` did not resolve g.LEDGERS. The reference implementation is the existing imported-constant callback and patch_policy.resolve_module, with the literal LEDGERS at scripts/new_host_grand_list.py:49 on merged HEAD 90a8c3f33. Local CPython 3.13.15 documents dict.items, dict.values and dict.keys as views and list/tuple iteration through __iter__. No network research or installation ran under the coordinator's offline constraint; no fresh upstream-SOTA verification is claimed. Native unittest remains the test executable.

Implementation: GateReads._attribute now resolves a previously unknown from-package module alias through the same imported-constant callback only when its attribute is a literal seq/map container. Existing iteration and destructuring consume those literals. Runtime-built or unresolved mappings keep the previous unknown value and legacy refusal; no refusal class changes. Other review fixes and the whole-runtime subject rule remain intact.

The TDD seam is the already-authorized public read derivation on the existing synthetic MemoryTree fixture, plus GateReads.reads on the caller alone. The latter prevents the helper's literal strings from masking a dropped loop read. The positive test covers dict.items tuple unpacking, the main-shaped dict comprehension, values, keys and plain imported list/tuple iteration, with exact files and a nonmatching path. The negative test covers an absent imported module and a module whose mapping is built by an unfollowed runtime call; both retain scripts/check.py:5 as unresolved and do not protect policy/strict.json.

Failing-first and corrections retained below:
- Initial test draft: exit 1, six positive subcase failures plus an overstrict negative assertion that required both legacy unresolved and unclassified reporting. The specified negative accepts either; the assertion was corrected to retain its existing unresolved result.
- Corrected failing-first suite: exit 1, six positive failures; the negative control passes before the source change.
- First implementation run: exit 1, six test-prefix assertion failures because the fixture's local composite action also protects tools/ci-action. That unrelated prefix assertion was replaced by the stronger caller-only exact-file check. No additional production-code repair was needed.
- Final focused suite: exit 0, both test methods pass, covering all eight subcases.
- Required full suite: exit 1, 202 tests in 44.753s, zero failures, one installed-gitleaks gitleaks_failed error and one skip. The repository test now passes, including zero unresolved reads, the exact dated unclassified list/count and unchanged breadth caps. This is locally authored integration/synthetic-fixture evidence, not upstream or live-provider acceptance.
